            <div id="main" class="container-12 clear-block">
      <div id="main-content" class="clear-block">
        <div class="stickycontainer clear-block">        <div id="main-column" class="grid-8">
        <div class="node"><h1>Slot Machine RTP</h1><div class="content"><div class="field field-type-filefield field-field-picture">
    <div class="field-items">
            <div class="field-item odd">
                    <img data-src="/sites/casinolistings.com/files/imagecache/newsimg/newsimg/2016/03/loose-slots-sign.jpg" alt="Loose Slots sign, Las Vegas" title="" width="620" height="348" loading="lazy" class="imagecache imagecache-newsimg imagecache-default imagecache-newsimg_default">        </div>
        </div>
</div>
<p><strong>Return to player</strong> (RTP) is a measure of how much a slot machine pays back to its players for each unit wagered over time. It is most commonly expressed as a percentage value and is almost always below 100%. The difference between 100% and the slot machine's RTP is known as the <strong>house edge</strong>, and this is the mathematical advantage that allows casinos to make a profit over the long term. Slot machines with a RTP percentage closer to 100% are often termed <strong>loose slots</strong> while games with a low RTP are referred to as <strong>tight</strong>.</p>

<p>For example, if you are playing a slot machine with a RTP value of 95%, the house edge is equal to 5%. This means that <em>on average and over millions of spins</em> the machine wins 5% of every unit wagered. Of course a game that accepted a $1 wager and paid back a 95c "win" on every spin would be hideously boring, not to mention impossible for a player to win against. Therefore slots are designed with paytables that give players a chance to win in the short term while still banking profits for their operators over the longer term and across a wide player base. As a player, picking a high RTP or loose slot (or at least avoiding the low RTP stinkers), will prolong your play sessions over the long term, so you can enjoy more spins for your budget and get better value for your money.</p>

<h2>RTP values for online slots</h2>

<p>The values in the table above have been documented from a variety of different sources, including documentation within the games themselves, data sheets provided by the game developers, and data disclosed by casinos that publish the RTP numbers for their games.</p>

<p>Some games have a RTP range with a significant difference between the maximum and minimum values. This can indicate one of a few possibilities such as a game that has different paytables that may be chosen and/or configured by the operator, a difference in payout based on the bet wagered by the player, or differences in bonus rounds that pay more or less based on choices made by players.</p>

<p>The RTP numbers for games can change over time due to software updates and changes made to the games by their developers. If you spot a game that you think has a wrong value in our list, <a href="/contact" rel="nofollow">please contact us</a> and we will update our database and throw in a CLchips reward for your trouble too.</p>

<h2>Effect of RTP on bonus value</h2>

<p>Almost all <a href="/bonuses">casino bonuses</a> allow play on slot machines, and in the vast majority of cases the wagering requirements on bonuses that allow different game types are lowest if you choose to play slots. It is useful to know that the relative value of a casino bonus can be affected by the slots that you choose to play and their RTP values. In short, you give yourself a better shot at winning and completing the bonus wagering requirements by picking slots with a higher RTP percentage.</p>

<p>As always, you should take care to read the terms and conditions associated with the bonus that you are claiming. Many casinos will disallow or devalue play on specific slots that are known to have a higher than average RTP. However as a rule of thumb, we have calculated the break-even points in terms of wagering requirements as a multiple of the bonus amount below. If you can find a bonus with a wagering requirement lower than the values in the table (or inside the yellow shaded area of the graph) below that allows you to play slots with the corresponding wagering requirement, then you will be playing with an advantage over the casino.</p>

<div style="overflow:hidden">
<div class="grid-3 alpha">
<table class="lister">
 <thead>
  <tr><th>RTP value</th><th class="number">Break-even <abbr title="wagering requirements">WR</abbr></th></tr>
 </thead>
 <tbody>
  <tr class="even"><td class="rtp active">98.0%</td><td class="number breakeven">50xB</td></tr>
  <tr class="odd"><td class="rtp active">97.0%</td><td class="number breakeven">33xB</td></tr>
  <tr class="even"><td class="rtp active">96.0%</td><td class="number breakeven">25xB</td></tr>
  <tr class="odd"><td class="rtp active">95.0%</td><td class="number breakeven">20xB</td></tr>
  <tr class="even"><td class="rtp active">94.0%</td><td class="number breakeven">17xB</td></tr>
  <tr class="even"><td class="rtp active">93.0%</td><td class="number breakeven">14xB</td></tr>
 </tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="grid-5 omega" style="margin-bottom:18px">
 <img src="/sites/casinolistings.com/files/reviews/2016/03/wager-reqs-vs-rtp.png" alt="Wager requirements vs RTP graph" title="Wager requirements vs RTP graph" width="380" height="252">
</div>
</div>

<p>For example, if you have found a bonus with a wagering requirement of 30xB (or 30 times the bonus amount), you cain gain an advantage by playing on slots with a RTP of around 96.7% or higher. If your bonus requires a higher wagering requirement of 40xB then you need to be playing slots at roughly 97.5% or better to maintain your advantage.</p>
